When the match is on T inside the body, the recursive calls in the branch are on A and B (subterms) — fine for direct recursion. For a mutual call on the same T = Ty_Arrow A B, the checker might require the argument to be a subterm of the original argument. `Ty_Arrow A B` is not a proper subterm of T in the syntactic sense... but it IS the same value; Coq's guard checker might see it as a reconstructed constructor and reject.

To avoid this, maybe define value_cond not by Fixpoint but as an inline match in LR:
```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  locally_closed t /\
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  (forall s, multi step t s -> value s ->
     match T with
     | Ty_Bool => s = tm_true \/ s = tm_false
     | Ty_Arrow A B => forall w, LR A w -> LR B (tm_app s w)
     end).
```
This is a single Fixpoint where LR (Arrow A B) t's body has the match with recursive calls LR A and LR B on subterms. Coq should accept this: the recursive occurrences are under the match on the principal argument T; in the Ty_Arrow A B branch, A and B are subterms. Actually hmm, the match on T appears *inside* the body after the conjunctions and inside a forall. Coq's guard checker handles matches on the recursive argument: when it sees `match T with ... end`, it refines T to the constructor patterns, and recursive calls on the pattern variables (A, B) are accepted. Yes, this is standard.

Let me define abbreviations:
```
Definition value_cond (T : ty) (s : tm) : Prop :=

  | Ty_Bool => s = tm_true \/ s = tm_false
  | Ty_Arrow A B => forall w, LR A w -> LR B (tm_app s w)

```
This Definition refers to LR (already defined). Note the definitional equality: `value_cond (Ty_Arrow A B) s` reduces to `forall w, LR A w -> LR B (app s w)`. ✓. But is `LR T t` usable as `lc t /\ SN t /\ (forall s, multi step t s -> value s -> value_cond T s)`? The body of LR uses an inline match, while value_cond uses a match in a Definition; when T is a variable, they're not convertible (both stuck). When T is a constructor, both reduce to the same. For proofs by destructing T, this is fine. For lemmas stated with value_cond, we can prove intro/inv lemmas by destructing T.

Let's just write:
```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  locally_closed t /\
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  (forall s, multi step t s -> value s ->
    match T with
    | Ty_Bool => s = tm_true \/ s = tm_false
    | Ty_Arrow A B => forall w, LR A w -> LR B (tm_app s w)
    end).
```
Coq guard check: the recursive calls LR A w and LR B (app s w) occur inside a match on T. The match is on the principal argument T (which is a variable in the Fixpoint). In the Ty_Arrow A B branch, A and B are variables bound by the pattern, subterms of T. Guard checker should accept.

Then:
```
Definition LR_bool_cond (t : tm) :=
  forall s, multi step t s -> value s -> s = tm_true \/ s = tm_false.
Definition LR_arrow_cond (A B : ty) (t : tm) :=
  forall s, multi step t s -> value s -> forall w, LR A w -> LR B (tm_app s w).
```
and lemmas:
```
Lemma LR_Bool_iff : forall t, LR Ty_Bool t <-> lc t /\ SN t /\ LR_bool_cond t.
Proof. intro t; simpl. reflexivity? Qed.
```
Hmm, `LR Ty_Bool t` unfolds to `lc t /\ SN t /\ (forall s, multi ... -> value s -> Ty_Bool branch)`. The branch is `s = true \/ s = false`; LR_bool_cond is the same. Should work? Actually `<->` is `iff P Q := (P -> Q) /\ (Q -> P)`; `reflexivity` on `P <-> P` works if P and Q are definitionally equal. Yes.

- LR_multi: iterates given steps.

- LR_if_gen: inverts given steps.

This is a much simpler development. Let me now carefully define everything and write the proofs.

**Definitions:**

```coq
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  (forall s, multi step t s -> value s ->
     match T with
     | Ty_Bool => s = tm_true \/ s = tm_false
     | Ty_Arrow A B => forall w, LR A w -> LR B (tm_app s w)
     end).
```

Wait: `strongly_normalizing` is defined *after* the typing rules in the file, and LR must be defined after it. Fine: we add our definitions after `strongly_normalizing` (before the theorem). The theorem statement is at the end; we can insert definitions/lemmas between the `strongly_normalizing` inductive and the `Theorem normalization`.

**Key helper definitions:**
```coq
Definition value_cond (T : ty) (s : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => s = tm_true \/ s = tm_false
  | Ty_Arrow A B => forall w, LR A w -> LR B (tm_app s w)
  end.
```
Hmm, but LR's body uses the inline match; value_cond is definitionally the same when T is a constructor. Let's just use the inline match in lemmas via destructing T.

Actually, maybe I can define LR with the match factored out using a helper that doesn't refer to LR... no, value_cond needs LR. Mutual fixpoint might work; let's test. If mutual fixpoint works, then `LR T t` would be definitionally `SN t /\ (forall s, multi ... -> value_cond T s)` for all T, even variables, which is much nicer! Let me try:

```coq
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  (forall s, multi step t s -> value s -> value_cond T s)
with value_cond (T : ty) (s : tm) {struct T} : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => s = tm_true \/ s = tm_false
  | Ty_Arrow A B => forall w, LR A w -> LR B (tm_app s w)
  end.
```
Guard condition: LR (Arrow A B) t calls value_cond (Arrow A B) s — mutual call with the same principal argument T. If mutual fixpoint fails, fall back to the inline match version (single Fixpoint), which is definitely accepted:
```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  (forall s, multi step t s -> value s ->
     match T with
     | Ty_Bool => s = tm_true \/ s = tm_false
     | Ty_Arrow A B => forall w, LR A w -> LR B (tm_app s w)
     end).
```
Then lemmas:
```
Lemma LR_bool_iff : forall t, LR Ty_Bool t <->
  strongly_normalizing t /\ (forall s, multi step t s -> value s -> s = tm_true \/ s = tm_false).
Proof. intro t; simpl. reflexivity. Qed.
```
Hmm, `simpl` needs to reduce `LR Ty_Bool t`. Since LR is a Fixpoint with argument T = Ty_Bool (constructor), it should reduce. Then the match reduces. `reflexivity` might need the RHS to be syntactically the same; the RHS as written has the same structure. Let's write lemmas in the "unfolded" form and use `cbn`.

Maybe a cleaner approach: define LR without match in the body by using an auxiliary Fixpoint on ty returning a function... 

Actually, here's another idea: define `value_cond` as a Fixpoint *after* LR, using LR, and prove `LR` is extensionally equal to a definition using value_cond:
```
Fixpoint value_cond (T : ty) (s : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => s = tm_true \/ s = tm_false
  | Ty_Arrow A B => forall w, LR A w -> LR B (tm_app s w)
  end.
```
Here value_cond refers to LR (defined before), and LR's body has the inline match. Then:
```
Lemma LR_unfold : forall T t, LR T t <->
  strongly_normalizing t /\ (forall s, multi step t s -> value s -> value_cond T s).
Proof. intros T t; destruct T; simpl; reflexivity? Qed.
```
For T = Ty_Bool: LR Ty_Bool t unfolds to the inline Bool match; value_cond Ty_Bool s unfolds to s = true \/ s = false. Definitionally equal ✓. For T = Arrow A B: both unfold to forall w, LR A w -> LR B (app s w) ✓. So destruct T; reflexivity should work (maybe with `unfold value_cond; simpl`). 

Then all subsequent lemmas can be stated in terms of value_cond, using LR_unfold.

```
Lemma step_app_inv : forall t1 t2 s,
  step (tm_app t1 t2) s ->
  (exists T body v, t1 = tm_abs T body /\ t2 = v /\ value v /\ s = open body v)

Lemma LR_app : forall A B t1 t2,
  LR (Ty_Arrow A B) t1 -> LR A t2 -> LR B (tm_app t1 t2).
Proof.
  intros A B t1 t2 H1 H2.
  assert (Hsn1 : strongly_normalizing t1) by (apply LR_sn; exact H1).
  revert H1 H2. revert t2? 
```
We need to induct on Hsn1 with motive `fun t1 => LR (A→B) t1 -> forall t2, LR A t2 -> LR B (app t1 t2)`. Let's set up:
```
  revert H1 H2. 
  (* goal: LR (Arrow A B) t1 -> forall t2, LR A t2 -> LR B (app t1 t2) *)
  induction Hsn1 as [t1 IH].
```
After induction, goal: `LR (Arrow A B) t1 -> forall t2, LR A t2 -> LR B (app t1 t2)` with IH : forall t1', step t1 t1' -> LR (Arrow A B) t1' -> forall t2, LR A t2 -> LR B (app t1' t2).
Then intros H1 t2 H2.
We need LR B (app t1 t2) = SN ∧ cond.
1. SN: SN_intro; intros s Hs; inversion Hs:
   - ST_AppAbs: t1 = abs T body, t2 = v; s = open body v. Use H1's value condition at t1: value t1 (from lc premise + v_abs), multi_refl: LR_value gives value_cond (Arrow A B) t1? LR_value H1 Hval : value_cond (Arrow A B) t1 = forall w, LR A w -> LR B (app t1 w). Apply to t2 (need t2 = v in this branch; H2 : LR A t2 ✓): LR B (app t1 t2). Then LR_sn gives SN(app t1 t2); SN_step with Hs gives SN s ✓.
   - ST_App1: t1 --> t1', t2 unchanged, s = app t1' t2. Apply IH with step, LR_step H1 step, H2: LR B (app t1' t2) → LR_sn ✓.
   - ST_App2: value t1, t2 --> t2', s = app t1 t2'. LR_value H1 Hval : value_cond (Arrow A B) t1 → forall w, LR A w -> LR B (app t1 w). Apply to t2' with LR A t2' from LR_step H2 step: LR B (app t1 t2') → LR_sn ✓.
2. Value condition: intros s Hm Hv. Need value_cond B s.
   destruct Hm as [x | x y z Hs Hm]; in refl: s = app t1 t2, contradiction with value via app_not_value.
   In step: Hs : step (app t1 t2) y, Hm : multi y s. Invert Hs:
   - ST_AppAbs: t1 = abs T body, t2 = v, y = open body v. H1's value condition: LR_value H1 Hval (value t1 from lc premise) → forall w, LR A w -> LR B (app t1 w) with w := t2: LR B (app t1 t2). Then LR_step with Hs gives LR B y (since y = open body v — wait, Hs : step (app t1 t2) y, so LR_step HLB Hs gives LR B y directly, no need to identify y!). Then LR_multi with Hm gives LR B s; LR_value gives value_cond B s (Hv) ✓.
     Nice: we don't need to identify the reduct.
   - ST_App1: t1 --> t1', y = app t1' t2. Apply IH with step and LR_step H1 step and H2: LR B y. Then LR_multi Hm → LR B s → value_cond ✓.
   - ST_App2: value t1, t2 --> t2', y = app t1 t2'. LR_value H1 Hval → value_cond (Arrow A B) t1 → with w := t2' and LR_step H2 step: LR B (app t1 t2') = LR B y. Then LR_multi Hm ✓.
   
Great, LR_app is provable with SN_ind on t1 and inversion on steps. The ST_AppAbs branch needs value t1, obtained from the step's lc premise:
```
Hlc : locally_closed (tm_abs T body) ⊢ value (tm_abs T body) via v_abs.
```

**LR_if_gen:**
```
Lemma SN_if : forall t1 t2 t3, SN t1 -> SN t2 -> SN t3 -> SN (tm_if t1 t2 t3).
Proof.
  intros t1 t2 t3 H1. revert t2 t3. 
  induction H1 as [t1 IH].
  intros t2 t3 H2 H3.
  apply SN_intro. intros s Hs.
  inversion Hs; subst...
  - ST_IfTrue: s = t1? Wait, constructor ST_IfTrue: forall t1 t2, lc t1 -> lc t2 -> tm_if tm_true t1 t2 --> t1. Matching tm_if t1 t2 t3: gives t1 = tm_true, s = t2. SN t2 ✓ (H2).
  - ST_IfFalse: t1 = tm_false, s = t3 ✓.
  - ST_If: t1 --> t1', s = tm_if t1' t2 t3. Apply IH with step and H2, H3 ✓.
```
But the induction on H1 : SN t1 with motive `fun t => SN (if t t2 t3)`? We revert t2 t3? The motive should be `fun t1 => forall t2 t3, SN t2 -> SN t3 -> SN (if t1 t2 t3)`. So:
```
  intros t1 t2 t3 H1. revert t2 t3.
  induction H1 as [t1 IH].
  intros t2 t3 H2 H3.
```
Then IH : forall t1', step t1 t1' -> forall t2 t3, SN t2 -> SN t3 -> SN (if t1' t2 t3). ✓.

Wait: `revert t2 t3` then `induction H1` — the goal is `forall t2 t3, SN t2 -> SN t3 -> SN (if t1 t2 t3)`, and H1 : SN t1. `induction H1` should generalize t1 (and thus the whole goal). The motive: fun t => forall t2 t3, SN t2 -> SN t3 -> SN (if t t2 t3). ✓.

```
Lemma LR_if : forall t1 T t2 t3,
  strongly_normalizing t1 -> LR T t2 -> LR T t3 -> LR T (tm_if t1 t2 t3).

  apply LR_intro.
  - apply SN_if.  (* SN t1, SN t2, SN t3 *)
    + admit? We need SN t1 — hmm, after induction we've lost SN t1! The induction principle gives only the IH, not the hypothesis SN t1.
```
Oops. With `strongly_normalizing_ind` (Acc-like), the case goal has only IH : forall t1', step t1 t1' -> P t1', not SN t1. But SN_if's proof needed SN t1 to do SN_intro? Actually to prove SN (if t1 t2 t3) with SN t1, we used induction on SN t1; the induction principle's IH suffices: SN_intro requires for all s, step (if...) s -> SN s:
- ST_IfTrue/False: SN t2/t3 ✓.
- ST_If: s = if t1' t2 t3, need SN of that. We don't have P t1' = SN (if t1' t2 t3)? If the motive P t1 := SN (if t1 t2 t3) (with t2,t3 fixed), then IH gives it. So SN_if can be proven with the motive `fun t => SN (if t t2 t3)` and no need for SN t1 in the case. But the induction principle requires... let me check: to use `strongly_normalizing_ind` with motive P, we need forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> P t') -> P t. Similarly for LR_if: motive `fun t1 => forall T t2 t3, LR T t2 -> LR T t3 -> LR T (if t1 t2 t3)`. Case: prove the forall; for a given T,t2,t3,H2,H3:

  - value condition: intros s Hm Hv; destruct Hm; refl → if_not_value contradiction; step → invert:

Wait, but in LR_if's SN part, when inverting `step (tm_if t1 t2 t3) s`, the ST_If case gives premise step t1 t1' and lc t2, lc t3; we use IH keyed on step t1 t1'. ✓.

Similarly, LR_app_abs's induction works with the motive, no need for the SN hypothesis in the case. Let's re-examine: motive P t2 := LR A t2 -> LR B (app (abs A body) t2). And LR_app: motive P t1 := LR (Arrow A B) t1 -> forall t2, LR A t2 -> LR B (app t1 t2). Case uses IH for t1' with step and LR_step. ✓.

Lemma SN_ind' : forall (P : tm -> Prop),
  (forall t, (forall t', step t t' -> P t') -> P t) ->

strongly_normalizing_ind :

  ```
  Lemma multi_abs_eq : forall A body s, multi step (tm_abs A body) s -> s = tm_abs A body.

  The rewrite lemma: 
  ```
  Lemma subst_open_fresh : forall σ x v t,
    ~ In x (fvs t) ->
    subst_all (update_subst σ x v) (open t (tm_fvar x)) = open (subst_all σ t) v.

  Prove:
  - `subst_all_open_rec`: subst_all σ' (open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) t) = open_rec 0 (subst_all σ' (tm_fvar x)) (subst_all σ' t) = open_rec 0 v (subst_all σ' t).
  - subst_all σ' t = subst_all σ t via extension and x ∉ fvs t.

**Fundamental theorem statement:**
```
Theorem fundamental : forall Γ t T, has_type Γ t T ->
  forall σ, (forall x S, Γ x = Some S -> LR S (σ x)) ->
  LR T (subst_all σ t).
Proof.
  intros Γ t T H. induction H as [...]?
```
The induction principle for has_type with motive `fun Γ t T => forall σ, valuation σ Γ -> LR T (subst_all σ t)`. The T_Abs case's IH needs to be `forall x Hx, forall σ, valuation σ (update Γ x T1) -> LR T2 (subst_all σ (open t1 (fvar x)))`. Will Coq generate that? The constructor T_Abs:
```
T_Abs : forall (L : list atom) Gamma T1 T2 t1,
  (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (tm_fvar x)) T2) ->
  has_type Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (Ty_Arrow T1 T2)
```
The recursive occurrence `has_type ... T2` is under `forall x, ~ In x L ->`. Coq's induction principle generation: it should produce an IH with the binders x and Hx, then the motive. I believe Coq handles nested recursive occurrences in products under a forall. E.g., the standard STLC typing induction in SF works exactly like this. Yes.

Writing `induction H` with the motive inferred from the goal `forall σ, valuation σ Γ -> LR T (subst_all σ t)` — Coq will need to generalize σ and the valuation. Let's do:
```
  intros Γ t T H.
  induction H as [ ... ].
```
Since the goal mentions Γ, t, T (the indices) and H, and we want the motive `fun Γ t T => forall σ, ...`, Coq's `induction H` should generalize the goal over all variables that depend on the indices. It usually works. If not, use `remember` or `revert`.

Let me now think about naming and the exact case proofs:

**T_Var**: Γ x = Some T, t = tm_fvar x. Goal `forall σ, valuation σ Γ -> LR T (subst_all σ (tm_fvar x))`. subst_all σ (fvar x) = σ x. So `intros σ Hval; apply Hval; exact H0` (the typing premise Γ x = Some T).

**T_Abs**: as above.

**T_App**: premise1 : has_type Γ t1 (Arrow T1 T2); premise2 : has_type Γ t2 T1. IH1 : forall σ, val σ Γ -> LR (Arrow T1 T2) (subst_all σ t1); IH2. Goal forall σ val, LR T2 (subst_all σ (tm_app t1 t2)) = LR T2 (tm_app (subst_all σ t1) (subst_all σ t2)). So intros σ Hval; apply LR_app; [apply IH1 | apply IH2]; assumption. ✓

**T_True/T_False**: LR Bool true: use LR_intro with SN true and value_cond. SN true: SN_intro no steps. value_cond Bool s: `s = true \/ s = false`; given multi true s and value s: since true doesn't step, s = true. Lemma `multi_true_eq : multi step tm_true s -> s = tm_true` (like multi_abs_eq). Then auto.
Similarly for false.

Wait, value_cond Bool s is `s = tm_true \/ s = tm_false`; given Hm : multi step tm_true s, we prove s = tm_true by `multi_value_eq`? General lemma: `multi_of_stuck`? For true: multi step tm_true s → s = tm_true. Prove by induction on Hm using true_no_step. Similarly false.

**T_If**: t = tm_if t1 t2 t3. IH1 : ... LR Bool (subst t1); IH2: LR T (subst t2); IH3. Goal LR T (if (subst t1) (subst t2) (subst t3)). Apply LR_if with SN (subst t1) from LR_sn IH1, then IH2, IH3. ✓

Wait, actually `LR_if`'s statement takes `strongly_normalizing t1`; we have LR Bool (t1σ) → LR_sn. ✓.

Now, one more check: **Does the T_Abs value condition require `LR_app_abs` with body := subst_all σ t1?** The goal's value_cond (Arrow T1 T2) s with s = tm_abs T1 bodyσ requires `forall w, LR T1 w -> LR T2 (tm_app (tm_abs T1 bodyσ) w)`. LR_app_abs T1 T2 bodyσ Hbody w HLRAw. Hbody : forall w, LR T1 w -> LR T2 (open bodyσ w) — proven inside. ✓.

**LR_app_abs details**: statement and proof:
```
Lemma LR_app_abs : forall A B body,
  (forall w, LR A w -> LR B (open body w)) ->
  forall t2, LR A t2 -> LR B (tm_app (tm_abs A body) t2).
Proof.
  intros A B body Hbody t2 HLRA.
  assert (Hsn : strongly_normalizing t2) by (apply LR_sn; exact HLRA).
  revert HLRA.
  induction Hsn as [t2 IH].
  intros HLRA.
  apply LR_intro.
  - (* SN *) apply SN_intro. intros s Hs.
    (* invert Hs : step (app (abs A body) t2) s *)
    ...
  - (* value cond *) intros s Hm Hv.
    destruct Hm as [x | x y z Hs Hm'].
    + (* refl: x = app ... = s *) exfalso; apply app_not_value ... 
    + (* step *) ...
Qed.
```
Hmm, in the SN part, after `apply SN_intro; intros s Hs`, we need to prove SN s. Inversion of Hs. The ST_AppAbs case: s = open body t2 (since t2 = v). We get Hbody t2 HLRA : LR B (open body t2) → LR_sn. In the ST_App1 case: impossible. ST_App2: Hval : value (abs A body), Hstep : step t2 t2', s = app (abs A body) t2'. Apply IH t2' Hstep (LR_step HLRA Hstep) : LR B (app (abs A body) t2') → LR_sn. 

But wait: the IH from `induction Hsn as [t2 IH]` has statement `forall t', step t2 t' -> LR A t' -> LR B (app (abs A body) t')` — actually the motive P t := LR A t -> LR B (app (abs A body) t), so IH : forall t', step t2 t' -> P t'. ✓.

In the value-cond part:
- refl: destruct Hm as [x | x y z Hs Hm']. In the refl case, x unifies with both `app (abs A body) t2` and s; so s = app ...; Hv : value (app ...) → contradiction. Need to substitute: `inversion Hm` might be better. Let's see: `destruct Hm as [x | x y z Hs Hm']`:

  - step: `multi_step x y z : R x y -> multi R y z -> multi R x z` unifies x = app..., z = s, y = ?; gives Hs : step (app...) y and Hm' : multi y s. 
- step case: invert Hs:

**Now, the fresh variable and `fvs`.** Let's design:

```
Fixpoint fvs (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvs t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2 ++ fvs t3
  end.
```

Fresh:
```
Fixpoint max_list (l : list atom) : atom :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0
  | x :: xs => Nat.max x (max_list xs)
  end.

Definition fresh (l : list atom) : atom := S (max_list l).

Lemma max_list_spec : forall l x, In x l -> x <= max_list l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| a l IH]; intros x Hin.
  - inversion Hin.
  - simpl. destruct Hin as [-> | Hin].
    + apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_list l); [apply IH; assumption | apply Nat.le_max_r].
Qed.

Lemma fresh_fresh : forall l, ~ In (fresh l) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. unfold fresh in Hin.
  apply max_list_spec in Hin. apply Nat.nlt_succ_diag_l?

**multi_abs_eq:**
```
Lemma multi_abs_eq : forall A body s, multi step (tm_abs A body) s -> s = tm_abs A body.

  induction Hm as [x | x y z Hs Hm IH]; intros E.
  - subst x. reflexivity.
  - subst x. exfalso. apply abs_no_step in Hs. exact Hs.
Qed.
```
This "remember" approach ensures the induction motive is general. Alternatively:
```
  induction Hm as [x | x y z Hs Hm IH].
  - reflexivity.
  - inversion Hs.
```
In the refl case, x = tm_abs A body and s = x, so reflexivity ✓. In the step case, x = tm_abs A body; Hs : step (tm_abs A body) y; goal z = tm_abs A body; `inversion Hs` closes it (no constructors). Qed.
```

Fixpoint max_list (l : list atom) : nat :=
  match l with
  | nil => 0

  end.

`Nat.max` exists.

max_list_spec:
```
Lemma max_list_spec : forall l x, In x l -> x <= max_list l.
Proof.
  induction l as [| a l IH]; intros x Hin.
  - inversion Hin.
  - simpl. destruct Hin as [Heq | Hin].
    + subst a. apply Nat.le_max_l.
    + apply Nat.le_trans with (max_list l).
      * apply IH; exact Hin.
      * apply Nat.le_max_r.
Qed.
```
`Nat.le_max_l : forall n m, n <= max n m`. ✓.

fresh:
```
Definition fresh (l : list atom) : atom := S (max_list l).
Lemma fresh_fresh : forall l, ~ In (fresh l) l.
Proof.
  intros l Hin. unfold fresh in Hin.
  apply max_list_spec in Hin.
  pose proof (Nat.lt_succ_diag_r (max_list l)). (* max < S max *)
  lia.
Qed.
```
Using lia requires `From Stdlib Require Import Lia.` Let me add that import at the top? Adding imports before the module or inside? We can add `From Stdlib Require Import Lia.` after the other imports, before the Module. But the instruction says don't modify supplied language definitions; adding imports is fine. Alternatively avoid lia:
```
  apply Nat.succ_le_iff in Hin? 
```
Let me check the exact names. There's `Nat.lt_succ_diag_r : forall n, n < S n`. From Hin : S (max_list l) <= max_list l and Hlt : max_list l < S (max_list l), we get a contradiction via `Nat.lt_le_trans`: max_list l < S max ≤ max_list l → max_list l < max_list l, contradicting `Nat.lt_irrefl`.
```
  assert (Hlt : max_list l < S (max_list l)) by apply Nat.lt_succ_diag_r.
  pose proof (Nat.lt_le_trans _ _ _ Hlt Hin) as Hc.
  exact (Nat.lt_irrefl _ Hc).
```
`Nat.lt_irrefl : forall n, ~ n < n`. ✓. This avoids lia.

```coq
(** * Auxiliary definitions and lemmas for the strong normalization proof *)

(* ---------- Free variables and substitution ---------- *)

Fixpoint fvs (t : tm) : list atom :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar _ => nil
  | tm_fvar x => x :: nil
  | tm_app t1 t2 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2
  | tm_abs _ t1 => fvs t1
  | tm_true => nil
  | tm_false => nil
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => fvs t1 ++ fvs t2 ++ fvs t3
  end.

Fixpoint subst_all (σ : atom -> tm) (t : tm) : tm :=
  match t with
  | tm_bvar i => tm_bvar i
  | tm_fvar x => σ x
  | tm_app t1 t2 => tm_app (subst_all σ t1) (subst_all σ t2)
  | tm_abs T t1 => tm_abs T (subst_all σ t1)
  | tm_true => tm_true
  | tm_false => tm_false
  | tm_if t1 t2 t3 => tm_if (subst_all σ t1) (subst_all σ t2) (subst_all σ t3)
  end.
```
Note the argument order: subst_all σ t. In the T_Abs case, `subst_all σ (tm_abs T t1)` reduces to `tm_abs T (subst_all σ t1)`.

Now the key lemmas list with statements:

```coq
Lemma subst_all_ext : forall t σ τ,
  (forall x, In x (fvs t) -> σ x = τ x) ->
  subst_all σ t = subst_all τ t.
Proof.
  induction t as [n | x | t1 t2 IH1 IH2 | T t1 IH1 | | | t1 t2 t3 IH1 IH2 IH3];
    intros σ τ Hagree; simpl.
  - reflexivity.
  - apply Hagree. simpl. left. reflexivity.
  - erewrite IH1; [erewrite IH2 | | ]; eauto using in_or_app.
    ...
```
Let me think about the app case more concretely. Goal after simpl:
`tm_app (subst_all σ t1) (subst_all σ t2) = tm_app (subst_all τ t1) (subst_all τ t2)`.
IH1 : forall σ τ, (forall x, In x (fvs t1) -> σ x = τ x) -> subst_all σ t1 = subst_all τ t1.
IH2 similar.
Apply f_equal2? `rewrite (IH1 σ τ); [rewrite (IH2 σ τ) | | ]; ...`. Coq's rewrite with explicit instantiation:
```
  - rewrite (IH1 σ τ). 
    + rewrite (IH2 σ τ). 
      * reflexivity.
      * intros x Hx. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. right. exact Hx.
    + intros x Hx. apply Hagree. simpl. apply in_or_app. left. exact Hx.
```
Wait, `rewrite (IH1 σ τ)` will ask for the premise as a side goal (unless we provide it). The order of goals after rewrite: the main goal first, then the side conditions? In Coq, `rewrite H` where H has premises generates the side goals after the main goal (usually). Actually, `rewrite` with a conditional lemma generates side goals, and the main goal is first? Let me recall: `rewrite H` where H : forall x, P x -> a = b. Coq will instantiate x, then produce two goals: the main goal rewritten, and the premise P ?x. I think the main goal comes first. But if a premise's variables can't be inferred, Coq may fail. To be safe, use `erewrite` or assert the equalities first:
```
  - assert (E1 : subst_all σ t1 = subst_all τ t1) by (apply IH1; intros x Hx; apply Hagree; simpl; apply in_or_app; left; exact Hx).
    assert (E2 : subst_all σ t2 = subst_all τ t2) by (apply IH2; intros x Hx; apply Hagree; simpl; apply in_or_app; right; exact Hx).
    rewrite E1, E2. reflexivity.
```
Cleaner.

For the abs case: goal `tm_abs T (subst_all σ t1) = tm_abs T (subst_all τ t1)`. IH1 : forall σ τ, (forall x, In x (fvs t1) -> σ x = τ x) -> ...; Hagree : forall x, In x (fvs (tm_abs T t1)) -> ...; fvs (tm_abs T t1) = fvs t1 by definition (simpl reduces). So:
```
  - rewrite (IH1 σ τ); [reflexivity |].
    intros x Hx. apply Hagree. simpl. exact Hx.
```
Wait, `simpl` on `fvs (tm_abs T t1)` gives `fvs t1`. So Hx : In x (fvs t1); apply Hagree; simpl; exact Hx. But careful: after `simpl` in the goal `In x (fvs (tm_abs T t1))`, it becomes `In x (fvs t1)`. ✓.

For the if case: fvs (if t1 t2 t3) = fvs t1 ++ fvs t2 ++ fvs t3. Note the parse: `fvs t1 ++ fvs t2 ++ fvs t3` is `(fvs t1 ++ fvs t2) ++ fvs t3` (left assoc). In the proof, `simpl` yields that. Use in_or_app: for t3, right of the outer app; for t2, left of outer then right of inner; for t1, left of outer then left of inner.

Hmm, this is getting detailed. Let me define helper lemma:

`subst_all_update_out`:
```
Lemma subst_all_update_out : forall σ x v t,
  ~ In x (fvs t) -> subst_all (update_subst σ x v) t = subst_all σ t.
Proof.
  intros σ x v t Hx. apply subst_all_ext.
  intros y Hy. apply update_subst_neq.
  intros Heq. subst y. apply Hx. exact Hy.
Qed.
```
Here subst_all_ext's statement: forall t σ τ, ... — instantiate with t, update_subst σ x v, σ. `apply subst_all_ext; intros y Hy; ...`. The goal after apply is `forall y, In y (fvs t) -> update_subst σ x v y = σ y`. Then update_subst_neq needs x <> y; intro Heq : x = y; subst; contradiction. ✓.

`subst_open_fresh`:
```
Lemma subst_open_fresh : forall σ x v t,
  ~ In x (fvs t) ->
  subst_all (update_subst σ x v) (open t (tm_fvar x)) =
  open (subst_all σ t) v.
Proof.
  intros σ x v t Hx.
  unfold open.
  rewrite subst_all_open_rec.
  rewrite update_subst_same.
  rewrite subst_all_update_out with (σ := σ) (x := x) (v := v); [reflexivity | exact Hx].
Qed.
```
Wait: `subst_all σ' (open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) t) = open_rec 0 (subst_all σ' (tm_fvar x)) (subst_all σ' t)`. `subst_all σ' (tm_fvar x)` = σ' x = update_subst σ x v x = v (update_subst_same). `subst_all σ' t = subst_all σ t` by subst_all_update_out (needs x ∉ fvs t). Then result `open_rec 0 v (subst_all σ t) = open (subst_all σ t) v` — but `open (subst_all σ t) v` is `open_rec 0 v (subst_all σ t)` by definition ✓. However, `open t (tm_fvar x)` unfolds to `open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) t`. In the proof, `unfold open` changes the goal's `open (subst_all σ t) v` too. Let me not unfold but rewrite:
```
  change (subst_all (update_subst σ x v) (open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) t) =
          open_rec 0 v (subst_all σ t)).
  rewrite subst_all_open_rec.
  rewrite update_subst_same.
  rewrite subst_all_update_out; [reflexivity | exact Hx].
```
Careful: `subst_all_open_rec` gives `subst_all σ' (open_rec 0 (tm_fvar x) t) = open_rec 0 (subst_all σ' (tm_fvar x)) (subst_all σ' t)`. The RHS we want is `open_rec 0 v (subst_all σ t)`. After rewriting `update_subst_same`, the term `subst_all σ' (tm_fvar x)` becomes `v`; and `subst_all σ' t` needs rewriting to `subst_all σ t` via subst_all_update_out. That lemma states `subst_all (update_subst σ x v) t = subst_all σ t`; the occurrence in the goal is `subst_all (update_subst σ x v) t`. ✓.

Potential issue: `open (subst_all σ t) v` in the goal vs `open_rec 0 v (subst_all σ t)` — `open` is a Definition, so `change` should work.

Now LR definitions. Let me write:

```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  (forall s, multi step t s -> value s ->
     match T with
     | Ty_Bool => s = tm_true \/ s = tm_false
     | Ty_Arrow A B => forall w, LR A w -> LR B (tm_app s w)
     end).

Definition value_cond (T : ty) (s : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => s = tm_true \/ s = tm_false
  | Ty_Arrow A B => forall w, LR A w -> LR B (tm_app s w)
  end.
```

Will Coq accept the Fixpoint? In the test it did (with the dummy True premise; without it should be the same). Let me double check that the match on T inside the forall is accepted with recursive calls LR A / LR B: yes, the test passed.

Then:
```
Lemma LR_unfold : forall T t,
  LR T t <-> strongly_normalizing t /\ (forall s, multi step t s -> value s -> value_cond T s).
Proof. intros T t. destruct T; simpl; reflexivity. Qed.

Lemma LR_sn : forall T t, LR T t -> strongly_normalizing t.
Proof. intros T t H. apply LR_unfold in H. tauto. Qed.

Lemma LR_cond : forall T t s, LR T t -> multi step t s -> value s -> value_cond T s.
Proof. intros T t s H Hm Hv. apply LR_unfold in H. tauto. Qed.

Lemma LR_value : forall T t, LR T t -> value t -> value_cond T t.
Proof. intros. apply LR_cond with (t := t); auto. apply multi_refl. Qed.

Lemma LR_intro : forall T t, strongly_normalizing t ->
  (forall s, multi step t s -> value s -> value_cond T s) -> LR T t.
Proof. intros T t H Hc. apply LR_unfold. split; assumption. Qed.

Lemma value_cond_arrow : forall A B s,
  value_cond (Ty_Arrow A B) s <-> (forall w, LR A w -> LR B (tm_app s w)).
Proof. intros. simpl. reflexivity. Qed.

Lemma value_cond_bool : forall s,
  value_cond Ty_Bool s <-> (s = tm_true \/ s = tm_false).
Proof. intros. simpl. reflexivity. Qed.
```

Check `LR_unfold`: `destruct T; simpl; reflexivity`. For T = Ty_Bool: LR Ty_Bool t reduces to `SN t /\ (forall s, multi ... -> value s -> (s = true \/ s = false))`; RHS `SN t /\ (forall s, multi ... -> value s -> value_cond Ty_Bool s)`; value_cond Ty_Bool s reduces to s = true \/ s = false. `simpl` should reduce both and `reflexivity` proves the iff? `reflexivity` on `P <-> P` — we tested this in the scratch and it worked (test compiled with `destruct T; simpl; reflexivity`). ✓.

But careful: the exact statement `LR_unfold` with `value_cond` — in the test it worked. ✓.

The fvar case of subst_all_open_rec: `subst_all sigma (open_rec k u (tm_fvar x))`. open_rec k u (fvar x) = fvar x. Then subst_all sigma (fvar x) = sigma x. RHS: open_rec k (subst_all sigma u) (subst_all sigma (fvar x)) = open_rec k (...) (sigma x). Since sigma x is an arbitrary term, open_rec k (subst_all sigma u) (sigma x) is NOT equal to sigma x — because sigma x might contain bvar k? Ah right! The commutation of substitution and opening fails when the substituted terms contain bvars with index k? Wait, no: subst_all replaces fvars; opening replaces bvars. These should commute... Let's recheck: open_rec k u (tm_fvar x) = tm_fvar x (no bvar to replace). subst_all sigma (tm_fvar x) = sigma x. RHS: open_rec k (subst_all sigma u) (subst_all sigma (tm_fvar x)) = open_rec k (subst_all sigma u) (sigma x). If sigma x contains a bvar with index k, then open_rec would replace it — but in the LHS, sigma x is inserted only after opening, so it's not opened. So the commutation fails in general! The correct statement must account for the fact that we substitute fvars and open bvars: opening after substitution affects the substituted terms only if they contain bvars — opening acts on bvars in the body t, not on the substituted σ x terms. Hmm.

Wait, but the formula `subst_all σ (open_rec k u t) = open_rec k (subst_all σ u) (subst_all σ t)` is a standard commutation; let's check with t = bvar i where i = k: LHS = subst_all σ u; RHS = open_rec k (σ u) (subst_all σ (bvar k)) = open_rec k (σ u) (bvar k) = σ u. ✓. For t = bvar i ≠ k: LHS = bvar i; RHS = open_rec k (σ u) (bvar i) = bvar i ✓. For t = fvar x: LHS = σ x; RHS = open_rec k (σ u) (σ x). If σ x contains bvar k, RHS ≠ σ x. So the commutation fails *because σ x may contain free bvars that accidentally coincide with the opening index*. 

Right: the correct commutation is with opening *with a variable* (fvar), or with σ mapping to locally closed terms. In our use, σ maps to terms in LR, but do those have bvars? σ x for x in Γ could be an arbitrary term with bvars? In the fundamental theorem, σ maps to LR terms; LR terms are... do they have bvars? They could (e.g., a term with a bvar is SN vacuously and might satisfy value conditions). Hmm. But in the actual proof, σ is built from arbitrary LR terms, which may contain bvars. However, the opening index k=0, and σ x terms... bvar 0 in σ x would be captured by `open_rec 0`. So the lemma is false as stated.

The right fix: generalize the substitution to handle the interaction correctly, or prove the commutation for terms where σ's images are locally closed (lc_at 0). In the fundamental theorem, the valuation maps to LR terms, and LR doesn't include lc. Hmm. We could add lc to LR after all, or we could restrict the substitution to *locally closed* images and prove lc for LR... Alternatively, formulate the open/subst commutation differently:

We want: `subst_all σ' (open t (fvar x)) = open (subst_all σ t) v` where σ' = update σ x v. Here x is inserted as an fvar, so `open t (fvar x)` has no bvars from the opening except bvars of t below the opening depth. Then substituting σ' maps fvars to terms; the RHS `open (subst σ t) v` opens the substituted body with v. If σ's images have bvars, there might be capture differences: In the LHS, `subst_all σ' (open_rec 0 (fvar x) t)`: the fvars of t (and x) are replaced by σ' images; bvar 0 of t was replaced by fvar x before substitution, so any bvar 0 occurrences in σ' images remain untouched. In the RHS, `open_rec 0 v (subst_all σ t)`: here bvars of t (including bvar 0) remain in subst_all σ t, and then opening replaces bvar 0 by v. But the σ images substituted for fvars in t might themselves contain bvar 0, which would then be wrongly captured by the outer open! So indeed the equation fails if σ images contain bvar 0.

So we need σ images to be locally closed (no bvars at all? or at least no bvars at the opening depth). In the fundamental theorem, the valuation images are LR terms. If we add locally_closed to LR, then all σ images are lc_at 0 (no bvars), and the commutation holds.

Alternatively, can we ensure σ images are locally closed without adding lc to LR? The valuation in the fundamental theorem is constructed from:
- Initial σ for the theorem: identity substitution (fvar x) — locally closed ✓.
- In T_Abs, σ' = update σ x v where v comes from LR A in the T_Abs case... Wait, in the fundamental theorem's T_Abs case, the valuation condition must hold for *arbitrary* valuations σ satisfying the LR condition. σ x for x ∈ Γ could be an arbitrary LR term, which might contain bvars. So we'd need LR → lc.

Hmm. But do LR terms contain bvars in practice? The definition of LR doesn't exclude them. If we add locally_closed to LR, we're back to needing lc preservation and has_type_lc etc. Alternatively, we can strengthen the induction hypothesis/fundamental theorem to quantify over valuations with locally closed images only, and prove that LR terms are locally closed... circular.

Alternatively: avoid `subst_all` with arbitrary σ by using a different substitution representation that doesn't capture. Since bvars and fvars are separate namespaces, why does capture occur? The issue is that substitution replaces fvars by terms that may contain bvars; those bvars are meant to be free in the substituted term but become bound by surrounding abstractions... Wait, no: in locally nameless, fvars are free variables; substituting an fvar by a term containing a bvar should be problematic: bvar 0 in the substituted term would refer to the enclosing binder, which is capture. In a proper locally nameless substitution, when substituting under an abstraction, you should not just recurse structurally because fvars are distinct from bvars — actually, fvars and bvars are distinct constructors, and substitution of fvars by arbitrary terms is NOT capture-avoiding if the terms contain bvars. Since fvars are free variables and bvars are bound variables, substituting a free variable x by a term u that contains a bvar i (which is bound by an enclosing binder in t) would cause capture. But in well-formed terms, u should be locally closed (no free bvars) when substituted for a free variable. Our valuation images should be locally closed. So adding lc to LR seems necessary after all, OR we can require the valuation to map to locally closed terms.

Wait — maybe there's another route: define substitution to shift? In standard locally nameless, substituting a free variable x with a term u under an abstraction doesn't require shifting because u's free variables are fvars, and bvars in u would indeed be captured. Standard LN substitution requires u to be locally closed. In SF's LN chapter, `subst x u t` is defined structurally and works fine because they only substitute locally closed terms. Yes.

So: LR must imply locally_closed. Let's revisit. We had earlier decided that adding lc to LR forces has_type_lc etc. But maybe there's a simpler way to get lc in the fundamental theorem: the fundamental theorem's conclusion LR T (subst_all σ t) needs lc of the substituted term, and the valuation gives lc of σ x. So we need substitution preserves lc. And has_type_lc for the T_Abs case. That's doable.

But wait — maybe we can avoid arbitrary valuations entirely by a different formulation: prove the fundamental theorem only for the *empty* context plus one variable? Hmm, but the T_Abs case requires a valuation for Γ extended with x. Γ is arbitrary. Since we're proving by induction on typing, Γ accumulates. So we need valuations for arbitrary contexts.

Alternatively, prove the fundamental theorem in a "substitution-free" style: interpret Γ as a list of terms and prove `has_type Γ t T -> (forall x, Γ x = Some S -> locally_closed (σ x) ...)`. We're back to valuations.

OK so add locally_closed to LR. Let's redo the design with lc.

Revised LR:
```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  locally_closed t /\
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  (forall s, multi step t s -> value s ->
     match T with
     | Ty_Bool => s = tm_true \/ s = tm_false
     | Ty_Arrow A B => forall w, LR A w -> LR B (tm_app s w)
     end).
```
value_cond unchanged.

Consequences:
1. LR_sn, LR_lc.
2. LR_intro takes lc.
3. LR_step needs step_lc.
4. LR_multi needs multi_lc (or iterate step_lc).
5. LR_app proofs: all conclusions now need lc of the app: from LR_lc of parts. No problem.
6. LR_app_abs: conclusion LR B (app (abs A body) t2) needs lc: lc (abs A body) and lc t2. We have lc t2 from LR_lc HLRA, but lc (abs A body) must be a hypothesis. Add it: `locally_closed (tm_abs A body)`.
7. In the fundamental theorem T_Abs, provide lc (abs T1 bodyσ) via has_type_lc + substitution lc.
8. The T_Abs Hbody: forall w, LR T1 w -> LR T2 (open bodyσ w). For LR_app_abs we need lc (abs T1 bodyσ) as a hypothesis. ✓.

```

lc_app : ...
lc_abs : forall k T t1, lc_at (S k) t1 -> lc_at k (abs T t1)
...
```
Induction on H : lc_at k t with motive `forall k', k <= k' -> lc_at k' t`:
- lc_bvar: i < k <= k' → i < k' ✓ via Nat.lt_le_trans.
- lc_fvar: lc_fvar k' x.
- lc_app: IH1, IH2 with k <= k' ✓.
- lc_abs: H : lc_at (S k) t1; IH : forall k'', S k <= k'' -> lc_at k'' t1. Goal lc_at k' (abs T t1) needs lc_at (S k') t1. Apply IH with S k' : need S k <= S k' from k <= k' ✓ (Nat.succ_le_mono).
- true/false/if: ✓.
Good.

12. `has_type_lc`:

Lemma has_type_lc : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> locally_closed t.
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H. induction H as [Gamma x T Hx
    | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hprem IH
    | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
    | Gamma | Gamma
    | Gamma t1 t2 t3 T H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3].
```
The induction principle for has_type with motive `fun Gamma t T => locally_closed t`:
- T_Var: lc_fvar.
- T_Abs: Hprem : forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (fvar x)) T2; IH : forall x Hx, locally_closed (open t1 (fvar x)). Choose x = fresh L; Hx : ~ In x L; IH x Hx : lc_at 0 (open t1 (fvar x)); lc_at_open_inv 0 t1 (fvar x) H (lc_fvar 0 x) : lc_at 1 t1; then lc_abs gives lc_at 0 (abs T1 t1). ✓.
  But careful: IH's exact statement depends on how Coq generates it. It might be `forall x, ~ In x L -> locally_closed (open t1 (fvar x))`. Let's verify with a test.
- T_App: IH1, IH2 → lc_app.
- T_True/T_False: lc_true/lc_false.
- T_If: lc_if.

Note: `lc_fvar : forall k x, lc_at k (tm_fvar x)`. ✓.

Lemma lc_at_bvar_inv : forall k n, lc_at k (tm_bvar n) -> n < k.
Proof. intros k n H; inversion H; subst; assumption. Qed.

Proof. intros k t1 t2 H; inversion H; subst; split; assumption. Qed.

Lemma lc_at_abs_inv : forall k T t1, lc_at k (tm_abs T t1) -> lc_at (S k) t1.
Proof. intros k T t1 H; inversion H; subst; assumption. Qed.

  lc_at k t1 /\ lc_at k t2 /\ lc_at k t3.
Proof. intros k t1 t2 t3 H; inversion H; subst; auto. Qed.
```

The forward direction: n <= m -> S n <= S m, whose conclusion `S n <= S m` matches the goal with n=k0, m=k'. ```

Now LR.

```
Fixpoint LR (T : ty) (t : tm) : Prop :=
  locally_closed t /\
  strongly_normalizing t /\
  (forall s, multi step t s -> value s ->
     match T with
     | Ty_Bool => s = tm_true \/ s = tm_false
     | Ty_Arrow A B => forall w, LR A w -> LR B (tm_app s w)
     end).

Definition value_cond (T : ty) (s : tm) : Prop :=
  match T with
  | Ty_Bool => s = tm_true \/ s = tm_false
  | Ty_Arrow A B => forall w, LR A w -> LR B (tm_app s w)
  end.
```
Note the Fixpoint's structural argument: T is the first argument; the recursive calls occur in the match on T's Arrow branch, so the guard checker should accept. In the test it did.

Will Coq complain about `LR` being defined with a Prop-valued match? Should be fine.

```
Lemma LR_unfold : forall T t,
  LR T t <-> locally_closed t /\ strongly_normalizing t /\
    (forall s, multi step t s -> value s -> value_cond T s).
Proof. intros T t. destruct T; simpl; reflexivity. Qed.
```
Hmm, destruct T produces T = Ty_Bool and T = Ty_Arrow t t0. In the Arrow case, the goal is:
LR (Ty_Arrow t t0) x <-> locally_closed x /\ SN x /\ (forall s, ... value_cond (Ty_Arrow t t0) s).
After simpl, both sides should reduce to the same. Let's test.

  (forall s, multi step t s -> value s -> value_cond T s) -> LR T t.

```
Lemma step_lc : forall t s, step t s -> locally_closed t -> locally_closed s.
Proof.
  intros t s H. induction H as [T t v Hlc Hval | t1 t1' t2 Hstep IH Hlc | v1 t2 t2' Hval Hstep IH
    | t1 t2 Hlc1 Hlc2 | t1 t2 Hlc1 Hlc2 | t1 t1' t2 t3 Hstep IH Hlc2 Hlc3];
    intros Hlc.
```
Careful about the step induction principle order. step constructors:
```
ST_AppAbs : forall T t v, lc (abs T t) -> value v -> app (abs T t) v --> open t v
ST_App1 : forall t1 t1' t2, t1 --> t1' -> lc t2 -> app t1 t2 --> app t1' t2
ST_App2 : forall v1 t2 t2', value v1 -> t2 --> t2' -> app v1 t2 --> app v1 t2'
ST_IfTrue : forall t1 t2, lc t1 -> lc t2 -> if true t1 t2 --> t1
ST_IfFalse : forall t1 t2, lc t1 -> lc t2 -> if false t1 t2 --> t2
ST_If : forall t1 t1' t2 t3, t1 --> t1' -> lc t2 -> lc t3 -> if t1 t2 t3 --> if t1' t2 t3
```
The induction principle interleaves recursive premise and IH: for ST_App1: t1, t1', t2, Hstep, IH, Hlc. For ST_App2: v1, t2, t2', Hval, Hstep, IH. For ST_If: t1, t1', t2, t3, Hstep, IH, Hlc2, Hlc3.

So pattern:
`[T t v Hlc Hval | t1 t1' t2 Hstep IH Hlc2 | v1 t2 t2' Hval Hstep IH | t1 t2 Hlc1 Hlc2 | t1 t2 Hlc1 Hlc2 | t1 t1' t2 t3 Hstep IH Hlc2 Hlc3]`.

Proof cases:
- ST_AppAbs: goal lc (open t v); Hlc : locally_closed (app (abs T t) v). Get lc abs and lc v via lc_at_app_inv; lc_at_abs_inv gives lc_at 1 t. Apply lc_at_open_rec_S with k=0: lc_at (S 0) t and lc_at 0 v → lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 v t). Note (S 0) vs 1: definitional ✓.
- ST_App1: Hlc : lc (app t1 t2) → lc t1, lc t2; IH applied to step t1 t1' and lc t1 → lc t1'; then lc_app.
- ST_App2: similar with t2.
- ST_IfTrue: Hlc : lc (if true t1 t2) → lc t1 (and lc t2); goal lc t1 ✓.
- ST_IfFalse: goal lc t2 ✓.
- ST_If: Hlc : lc (if t1 t2 t3) → lc t1,t2,t3; IH on step t1 t1' with lc t1 → lc t1'; lc_if.

```
This is `A \/ B \/ C` = `A \/ (B \/ C)` because `\/` is right-associative! So the top-level disjunction has 2 branches: A and (B \/ C). My pattern with 3 top-level branches is wrong. I need:
```
[[A0 [body [v [Eq1 [Eq2 [Hval Eqs]]]]]] | [t1' [Hstep Eqs]] | [t2' [Hval [Hstep Eqs]]]]
```
Coq's disjunctive pattern for `A \/ (B \/ C)` is `[patA | patB | patC]`? The error says expects 2 branches, meaning for the outer `\/` it expects 2; the nested `\/` requires nesting: `[patA | [patB | patC]]`. So I should write:
```
destruct (...) as
  [[A0 [body [v [Eq1 [Eq2 [Hval Eqs]]]]]] |
   [[t1' [Hstep Eqs]] | [t2' [Hval [Hstep Eqs]]]]].
```
And then the cases would be bullets: + for A, + for B, + for C (destruct on the nested pattern still creates 3 goals? With a nested disjunctive pattern, Coq creates 3 subgoals, and the bullets + + + work).

For route: The `\/` is right associative, so A \/ B \/ C parses as A \/ (B \/ C). In step_app_inv's statement I wrote with line breaks; yes right-assoc.

**subst_all_lc_at_fvs** (already planned):
```
Lemma subst_all_lc_at_fvs : forall sigma k t,
  lc_at k t ->
  (forall x, In x (fvs t) -> locally_closed (sigma x)) ->
  lc_at k (subst_all sigma t).
Proof.
  intros sigma k t Hlc. revert k.
  induction t as [n | x | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 | T t1 IH1 | | | t1 IH1 t2 IH2 t3 IH3];
    intros k Hlc Hcl; simpl.
  - (* bvar *) apply lc_bvar. apply lc_at_bvar_inv in Hlc. exact Hlc.
  - (* fvar *) apply lc_at_mono with (k := 0); [apply Hcl; simpl; left; reflexivity | apply Nat.le_0_l].
  - (* app *) apply lc_at_app_inv in Hlc. destruct Hlc as [H1 H2]. apply lc_app.
    + apply IH1; [exact H1 | intros y Hy; apply Hcl; simpl; apply in_app_l; exact Hy].
    + apply IH2; [exact H2 | intros y Hy; apply Hcl; simpl; apply in_app_r; exact Hy].
  - (* abs *) apply lc_at_abs_inv in Hlc. apply lc_abs. apply IH1; [exact Hlc | intros y Hy; apply Hcl; simpl; exact Hy].
  - apply lc_true.
  - apply lc_false.
  - (* if *) apply lc_at_if_inv in Hlc. destruct Hlc as [H1 [H2 H3]]. apply lc_if.
    + apply IH1; [exact H1 | intros y Hy; apply Hcl; simpl; apply in_app_l; exact Hy].
    + apply IH2; [exact H2 | intros y Hy; apply Hcl; simpl; apply in_app_r; apply in_app_l; exact Hy].
    + apply IH3; [exact H3 | intros y Hy; apply Hcl; simpl; apply in_app_r; apply in_app_r; exact Hy].
Qed.
```
Order of arguments: I wrote forall sigma k t but the induction on t with k reverted... Let me structure: `intros sigma k t Hlc. revert k. induction t ...; intros k Hlc Hcl.` Hmm, the hypothesis Hcl depends on t only, fine.

Wait: the statement order: `forall sigma k t, lc_at k t -> ...`. After intros sigma k t Hlc; revert k; induction t; intros k Hlc Hcl. The motive: fun t => forall k, lc_at k t -> (forall x, In x (fvs t) -> lc (sigma x)) -> lc_at k (subst_all sigma t). ✓.

**has_type_lc:**
```
Lemma has_type_lc : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T -> locally_closed t.
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H.
  induction H as [Gamma x T Hx
    | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hprem IH
    | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
    | Gamma
    | Gamma
    | Gamma t1 t2 t3 T H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3].
```
The induction principle for has_type: constructors:
```
T_Var : forall Gamma x T, Gamma x = Some T -> has_type Gamma (tm_fvar x) T
T_Abs : forall (L : list atom) Gamma T1 T2 t1,
  (forall x, ~ In x L -> has_type (update Gamma x T1) (open t1 (tm_fvar x)) T2) ->
  has_type Gamma (tm_abs T1 t1) (Ty_Arrow T1 T2)
T_App : forall Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2, has_type Gamma t1 (Arrow T1 T2) -> has_type Gamma t2 T1 -> has_type Gamma (app t1 t2) T2
T_True : forall Gamma, has_type Gamma tm_true Ty_Bool
T_False : forall Gamma, has_type Gamma tm_false Ty_Bool
T_If : forall Gamma t1 t2 t3 T, has_type Gamma t1 Ty_Bool -> has_type Gamma t2 T -> has_type Gamma t3 T -> has_type Gamma (if t1 t2 t3) T
```
The induction principle interleaves premises and IHs:
- T_Var: Gamma, x, T, Hx (no recursive premise) — pattern [Gamma x T Hx].
- T_Abs: L, Gamma, T1, T2, t1, Hprem, IH — pattern [L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hprem IH].
- T_App: Gamma, t1, t2, T1, T2, H1, IH1, H2, IH2.
- T_True: Gamma.
- T_False: Gamma.
- T_If: Gamma, t1, t2, t3, T, H1, IH1, H2, IH2, H3, IH3.

Proof:
- T_Var: lc_fvar.
- T_Abs: choose x := fresh (L ++ fvs t1). Then Hx : ~ In x L; IH x Hx : locally_closed (open t1 (fvar x)). lc_at_open_inv 0 t1 (fvar x) H (lc_fvar 0 x) gives lc_at 1 t1. Then lc_abs.
```
  - set (x := fresh (L ++ fvs t1)).
    assert (Hxin : ~ In x (L ++ fvs t1)) by (unfold x; apply fresh_fresh).
    assert (HxL : ~ In x L) by (intro Hin; apply Hxin; apply in_or_app; left; exact Hin).
    pose proof (IH x HxL) as Hopen.
    apply lc_abs.
    apply lc_at_open_inv with (u := tm_fvar x).
    + exact Hopen.
    + apply lc_fvar.
```
Careful: lc_at_open_inv : forall k t u, lc_at k (open_rec k u t) -> lc_at k u -> lc_at (S k) t. Goal lc_at 1 t1 = lc_at (S 0) t1. `apply lc_at_open_inv with (u := tm_fvar x)`: unify k=0, t=t1, conclusion lc_at (S 0) t1 ✓. Subgoals: lc_at 0 (open_rec 0 (fvar x) t1) = lc_at 0 (open t1 (fvar x)) [Hopen] and lc_at 0 (fvar x) [lc_fvar 0 x]. But `open t1 (tm_fvar x)` unfolds to open_rec 0 ... ✓.
Hmm, `apply lc_abs` on goal locally_closed (tm_abs T1 t1) = lc_at 0 (abs T1 t1): lc_abs gives premise lc_at (S 0) t1 ✓.
- T_App: lc_app with IH1, IH2.
- T_True/False: lc_true/lc_false.
- T_If: lc_if IH1 IH2 IH3.

```
Lemma fundamental : forall Gamma t T, has_type Gamma t T ->
  forall sigma, valuation sigma Gamma -> LR T (subst_all sigma t).
Proof.
  intros Gamma t T H.

    | L Gamma T1 T2 t1 Hprem IH
    | Gamma t1 t2 T1 T2 H1 IH1 H2 IH2
    | Gamma
    | Gamma
    | Gamma t1 t2 t3 T H1 IH1 H2 IH2 H3 IH3].
